Biliary Atresia

Complex MONDO:0008867 Pathograph 68 Show in embeddings browser Neonatal Cholestasis

Biliary atresia is an infantile fibro-obliterative cholangiopathy that obstructs bile drainage and causes conjugated hyperbilirubinemia, pale stools and progressive liver disease. Injury commonly begins before visible jaundice, and abnormal direct/conjugated bilirubin can be present shortly after birth. Clinical presentations include isolated disease, laterality-associated malformations and cystic biliary remnants. Genetic susceptibility, developmental abnormalities and dysregulated immune responses contribute to mechanistic models, while specific viral or toxin causes remain unproven in humans. Kasai portoenterostomy can restore drainage and prolong native-liver survival, but many patients develop continuing fibrosis, portal hypertension or recurrent cholangitis and require liver transplantation.

Inheritance

1
Non-Mendelian / multifactorial HP:0001426
Usually sporadic and non-Mendelian. A systematic review of 35 reported twin pairs found 34 discordant pairs, but published cases do not provide a population recurrence or penetrance estimate. Common susceptibility alleles and rare candidate variants may contribute. Biallelic PKD1L1 variants in five of 67 broadly defined BASM cases support a candidate recessive contribution in some patients, not an established Mendelian explanation for BASM as a whole.
Non-Mendelian inheritance
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:32504124 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"97.1% twins were discordant, among 55.9% of which were monozygotic twin sets, indicating that BA may be related to genetic phenotype or penetrance."
Published-case synthesis, with marked twin discordance; it does not estimate population penetrance or prove a particular environmental or epigenetic cause.
PMID:30664273 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"Five BASM subjects harbored rare and potentially deleterious biallelic variants in polycystic kidney disease 1 like 1 (PKD1L1), a gene associated with ciliary calcium signaling and embryonic laterality determination in fish, mice, and humans."
Candidate biallelic variants in a minority of the ascertained BASM cohort.
◆

Subtypes

5
Isolated (non-syndromic) biliary atresia
Biliary atresia without a recognized associated malformation syndrome, comprising most cases. The historical term perinatal form describes presentation, not proof of postnatal acquisition: direct/conjugated bilirubin may already be abnormal in the first days of life.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34884377 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"In the majority of cases, currently termed isolated BA, there are still too few clues as to aetiology or indeed pathogenesis."
Review synthesis of this clinical subgroup; definitions and overlap vary.
Biliary atresia splenic malformation syndrome MONDO:0016577
Biliary atresia with splenic and other laterality-associated malformations, including polysplenia or asplenia, abnormal visceral situs, intestinal malrotation, preduodenal portal vein and interrupted inferior vena cava. Definitions vary between cohorts. It accounts for roughly 10–15% in some Western series but is much less frequent in some Asian series; prognosis depends on associated anatomy and clinical context.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34884377 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The commonest of these is the Biliary Atresia Splenic Malformation (BASM) syndrome, which accounts for about 10–15% of European and American series ..., but is distinctly rare in Chinese and Japanese series (<2%) ...."
Review synthesis of this clinical subgroup; definitions and overlap vary.
Cystic biliary atresia
Biliary atresia with cystic dilatation of part of the extrahepatic remnant, sometimes detected prenatally. It must be distinguished from a choledochal cyst. Timely Kasai surgery often gives better jaundice-clearance outcomes than other biliary-atresia variants, although late deterioration can still occur.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34884377 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Post-operatively, these infants have >75% chance of clearance of jaundice and native liver preservation, though their prognosis does appear to have a marked relationship with age at surgery ...."
Review synthesis of this clinical subgroup; definitions and overlap vary.
Cytomegalovirus-associated biliary atresia
Clinical subgroup with evidence of CMV infection at presentation, often defined by CMV IgM. Associations with more severe disease and poorer outcomes have been reported, but seropositivity at diagnosis does not establish congenital infection, timing, or viral causation of biliary atresia.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34884377 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Therefore, using CMV IgM antibodies as a marker of infection up to 30% of Chinese BA series have been positive ... compared to about 10% in a UK series ...."
Review synthesis of this clinical subgroup; definitions and overlap vary.
Cardiac-associated biliary atresia
A clinical risk subgroup with cardiac anomalies, partly overlapping BASM. The phenotype is heterogeneous and does not establish one common pathogenesis; hemodynamically important heart disease affects operative planning and prognosis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34884377 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Indeed, when we reviewed our experience of biliary atresia-associated cardiac anomalies (n = 37), features indicative of BASM were present in 48% ...."
Review synthesis of this clinical subgroup; definitions and overlap vary.
⚙

Pathophysiology

25
Cholangiocyte Injury of the Bile Duct Epithelium
Cholangiocyte epithelial damage is a central lesion. RRV infection and biliatresone exposure produce experimentally defined injury in mice and zebrafish, respectively; neither demonstrates the initiating cause in human disease. Immune attack and developmental susceptibility may contribute through distinct, incompletely resolved routes.

Animal models
Rhesus rotavirus (RRV) neonatal mouse model Mouse partially recapitulates Early infection produces biliary epithelial injury and obstruction.
Biliatresone zebrafish model Zebrafish recapitulates Biliatresone reproduces selective extrahepatic-cholangiocyte injury, modeling the toxin route to the cholangiocyte-injury node.
Low-dose prenatal biliatresone mouse exposure Mouse fails to recapitulate The tested prenatal exposure did not induce the histologic injury phenotype.
Downstream
Immune-Mediated Periductal Inflammation Epithelial injury participates in the fibroinflammatory response; initial human triggers remain uncertain.
Ductular Epithelial Expansion A reactive epithelial response accompanies obstructive injury; lineage origin remains partly inferred.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23844248 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"the amount of cholangiocyte epithelial damage and bile duct edema was significantly greater in the early infection group"
Histologic epithelial injury in the time-dependent RRV mouse model.
PMID:27102575 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Biliatresone has selective toxicity for extrahepatic cholangiocytes (EHCs) in zebrafish larvae."
The plant toxin biliatresone selectively injures extrahepatic cholangiocytes, demonstrating that a targeted cholangiocyte insult can initiate a BA-like extrahepatic cholangiopathy.
Immune-Mediated Periductal Inflammation
Periductal innate and adaptive immune activation accompanies biliary epithelial injury. Human tissue studies show inflammatory infiltrates and regulatory abnormalities; perturbation studies in neonatal RRV mice support contributions from Th1, Th17, B-cell and macrophage pathways. No single experimental immune mechanism establishes the cause of all human cases.

Portal Mesenchymal Cell Activation Immune populations associate spatially with fibrotic tissue; inferred profibrotic signaling requires functional validation.

PMID:32518681 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Mild lymphocytic inflammation is usually present within portal tracts in BA. Other inflammatory cells, including eosinophils, plasma cells and macrophages, are also present."
Human pathology synthesis describes mixed portal inflammation.
PMID:36333281 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"Immune subpopulations inhabiting the 'fibrotic niche' (areas of scarring), comprising 'intermediate' CD14++ CD16+ monocytes, scar-associated macrophages, natural killer T cells, transitional B cells and FCN3+ neutrophils were identified."
Spatial association of immune populations with scar tissue, not a causal intervention.
Portal Mesenchymal Cell Activation
Sustained inflammation activates portal fibroblasts and transdifferentiates hepatic stellate cells into a myofibroblast-like, matrix-secreting phenotype — the organ-specific mesenchymal activation step of the conserved fibrotic response in the biliary atresia portal tract.

Downstream
Fibro-obliteration of the Biliary Tree Activated mesenchymal cells contribute excess matrix to duct scarring.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32518681 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The transformation and proliferation of hepatic stellate cells (HSCs) from fat-storing cells to myofibroblasts in BA plays an important role in hepatic fibrogenesis. HSCs are responsible for increased extracellular matrix, which leads to progressive scarring"
The pathology review supports mesenchymal activation and matrix production in biliary atresia.
Fibro-obliteration of the Biliary Tree
Activated portal myofibroblasts deposit excess extracellular matrix around and within the injured ducts, producing a fibro-obliterative process that progressively narrows and obliterates the extrahepatic biliary tree and damages the intrahepatic ducts, forming the characteristic fibrous remnant at the porta hepatis. This obliteration is the anatomic substrate that the Kasai portoenterostomy attempts to bypass.
collagen fibril organization GO:0030199 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves increased collagen fibril organization (GO:0030199). GO:0030199 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. ↑ INCREASED
Downstream
Impaired Bile Drainage Scarring obstructs the biliary lumen.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:32518681 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Histopathological findings in liver biopsies include the expansion of the portal tracts, with edematous fibroplasia and bile ductular proliferation, with bile plugs in duct lumen."
Portal-tract fibroplasia and ductular reaction on biopsy evidence the fibro-obliterative remodeling of this node.
PMID:32518681 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The destructive fibroinflammatory process that underlies BA, may involve a short segment of a duct, an entire duct or the entire biliary system."
Fibroinflammatory remodeling of the resected biliary tree.
Impaired Bile Drainage
Obstruction of the biliary tree reduces delivery of bile to the intestine and retains conjugated bilirubin and bile acids. Drainage may remain impaired after Kasai, while ongoing intrahepatic disease contributes to later liver injury.

"Biliary atresia is a progressive fibrosing obstructive cholangiopathy of the intrahepatic and extrahepatic biliary system, resulting in obstruction of bile flow and neonatal jaundice."
Obstructive bile-flow impairment and neonatal jaundice, distinct from subsequent cirrhotic remodeling.
Embryonic Patterning Disturbance
The combination of biliary atresia with splenic, visceral-situs, vascular and cardiac malformations suggests disturbed embryonic patterning in BASM. CFC1, a NODAL coreceptor, and PKD1L1 are candidate links to left-right signaling in a subset, with supportive animal data; the underlying defect remains unresolved in most patients. The molecular-function annotation identifies the linked protein’s normal activity; it does not establish the functional effect of each human susceptibility allele.

Disrupted Biliary Morphogenesis
Susceptibility-gene perturbations can impair developing biliary networks. Gpc1, add3a, arf6 and pkd1l1 studies provide distinct experimental routes, but sparse fish ducts are not equivalent to the human fibro-obliterated extrahepatic remnant. The molecular-function annotation identifies the linked protein’s normal activity; it does not establish the functional effect of each human susceptibility allele.

Altered ADD3 Regulatory Activity
At the human ADD3 susceptibility locus, H69 cholangiocyte reporter assays detect allele-dependent activity in four fragments: risk haplotypes increase activity at E2, E4 and E10 but decrease it at E9. The fragment containing lead marker rs17095355 has no significant allelic difference. These assays concern cloned regulatory elements; they do not establish endogenous expression changes, a causal nucleotide, or chromatin contacts in patient cholangiocytes. The molecular-function annotation identifies the linked protein’s normal activity; it does not establish the functional effect of each human susceptibility allele.

Independent dosage perturbations provide a plausible bridge to biliary development, not the effect of the assayed human variants in their native locus.
Excess Hedgehog Signaling in Developmental Models
Gpc1 or add3a knockdown increases Hedgehog-pathway readouts in zebrafish. Cyclopamine partially rescues the associated biliary defects, supporting a model-specific contribution of excessive signaling. A uniform Hedgehog abnormality caused by each human susceptibility allele has not been demonstrated. The molecular-function annotation identifies the linked protein’s normal activity; it does not establish the functional effect of each human susceptibility allele.

Reduced EGFR-ARF6 Developmental Signaling
Arf6 knockdown and EGFR inhibition produce sparse intrahepatic biliary networks and impaired bile transport in zebrafish, with arf6 mRNA rescue. The human noncoding association does not directly establish deficient ARF6 expression or this complete pathway in affected infants. The molecular-function annotation identifies the linked protein’s normal activity; it does not establish the functional effect of each human susceptibility allele.

Impaired Extrahepatic Duct Elastic-Fiber Organization
Adult Efemp1 heterozygous mouse ducts have fewer organized elastic fibers despite preserved total elastin, with altered responses to pressure. This suggests structural susceptibility involving fibulin-3, but neither spontaneous atresia nor the direction of effect of human EFEMP1 risk variants was established. A mechanical contribution to epithelial vulnerability remains a hypothesis without a demonstrated causal edge to injury. The molecular-function annotation identifies the linked protein’s normal activity; it does not establish the functional effect of each human susceptibility allele.

Measured duct mechanics following heterozygous gene loss.
Reduced Cholangiocyte Glutathione Reserve
In zebrafish, extrahepatic cholangiocytes have a relatively oxidized glutathione state and are susceptible to biliatresone-induced redox stress. Glutathione depletion sensitizes biliary cells, whereas NAC supplementation or Nrf2 activation reduces injury. This is a toxin-model mechanism; intrinsic glutathione deficiency has not been established as a general initiating human defect.
glutathione metabolic process GO:0006749 Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves glutathione metabolic process (GO:0006749). GO:0006749 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Animal models
Biliatresone zebrafish model Zebrafish perturbs Glutathione depletion increases toxin susceptibility, while antioxidant manipulations reduce injury.

Cholangiocyte Injury of the Bile Duct Epithelium Lower reserve sensitizes fish cholangiocytes to electrophilic toxin injury.

PMID:27102575 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"GSH depletion sensitized both EHCs and the otherwise resistant intrahepatic cholangiocytes to the toxin"
Manipulating antioxidant reserve changes susceptibility in fish.
Reduced Regulatory T-Cell Restraint
Human BA samples show reduced Treg frequency and inadequate CTLA-4 upregulation, including blood collected at transplantation; these observations do not establish an initiating neonatal defect. Treg augmentation after infection improves survival and biliary injury in the RRV mouse model, supporting a regulatory contribution to ongoing inflammation.
regulatory T cell CL:0000815 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves regulatory T cell (CL:0000815). CL:0000815 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.

Downstream
Immune-Mediated Periductal Inflammation Treg augmentation reduces model inflammation; the human initiating role is unproven.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:39131670 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"Patients with BA had decreased peripheral blood Treg frequency and lack of cytotoxic T lymphocyte-associated antigen-4 (CTLA-4) upregulation despite a highly activated, effector Treg phenotype."
Human clinical sample phenotype, with stage and comparator limitations.
PMID:39131670 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"Treg augmentation in the murine model led to increased survival, decreased direct bilirubin levels and liver inflammation, and expansion of resident macrophages."
Intervention result in RRV mice, not established human treatment efficacy.
Th1-Associated Cytotoxic Inflammation
T-bet-dependent inflammatory and cytotoxic programs contribute to duct injury in the RRV mouse model. T-bet deletion reduces these programs and preserves duct patency in many mice, but cholestatic symptoms and mortality persist. Duct patency therefore does not establish complete disease rescue.
Animal models
T-bet-deficient RRV mouse Mouse perturbs T-bet loss preserves duct patency despite continued obstructive-cholangiopathy symptoms and unchanged mortality.
Downstream
Cholangiocyte Injury of the Bile Duct Epithelium T-bet perturbation alters biliary injury without rescuing survival.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34943969 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"Even though the percentage of T-bet KO mice displaying symptoms of an obstructive cholangiopathy and overall mortality rate was not different compared to wild-type mice, the extrahepatic bile ducts of T-bet KO mice remained patent."
The primary study distinguishes anatomic protection from survival benefit.
Dendritic-Cell-Dependent Th17 Polarization
In the post-obstructive phase of RRV-induced disease, dendritic cells promote Th17 responses. Depletion decreases hepatic IL-17A and intrahepatic injury without preventing the initial extrahepatic obstruction. Human IL-17A-positive-cell and macrophage associations support relevance but do not constitute equivalent intervention evidence.

Cholangiocyte CCL2 Induction
IL-17A induces CCL2 expression in cultured neonatal cholangiocytes. In this assay it does not directly kill the biliary cells; the proposed injury pathway involves recruitment of inflammatory cells.
intrahepatic cholangiocyte CL:0002538 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves intrahepatic cholangiocyte (CL:0002538). CL:0002538 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.

CCR2-Dependent Inflammatory Macrophage Recruitment CCL2 signaling recruits CCR2-positive inflammatory macrophages in the model.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27641439 SUPPORT DIRECT In Vitro
"Recombinant IL-17A induced expression of chemokine (C-C motif) ligand 2 in neonatal cholangiocytes in vitro"
Direct cell-culture response to IL-17A.
CCR2-Dependent Inflammatory Macrophage Recruitment
CCL2/CCR2 signaling recruits inflammatory Ly6C-high macrophages during later RRV-model injury. CCR2 blockade reduces recruitment, and disrupted IL-17 signaling preserves intrahepatic ducts and improves survival. In human tissue, IL-17A-positive cells correlate with CD68-positive infiltration; causal human intervention evidence is lacking.
macrophage CL:0000235 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves macrophage (CL:0000235). CL:0000235 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Downstream
Immune-Mediated Periductal Inflammation Recruitment expands the model inflammatory infiltrate.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:27641439 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"blockade of the corresponding chemokine (C-C motif) receptor 2 reduced recruitment of inflammatory macrophages to the liver in vivo."
In vivo perturbation of the recruitment pathway.
PMID:27641439 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"the prevalence of IL-17A+ cells was positively correlated with the degree of CD68+ macrophage infiltration at diagnosis."
Human tissue association, not a test of CCR2 blockade.
Macrophage CXCL2-Dependent Neutrophil Chemotaxis
RRV-infected RAW264.7 macrophage conditioned medium recruits neutrophils, and CXCL2/Mip2 depletion abolishes this response. RRV is detected in hepatic macrophages in vivo, but the necessity of this chemotactic pathway for duct injury in vivo was not demonstrated. Infected cholangiocyte conditioned medium did not reproduce the response.

CL:0000235 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology. neutrophil CL:0000775 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves neutrophil (CL:0000775). CL:0000775 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Experimental models
RRV-infected macrophage conditioned-medium assay CELL LINE Tests the CXCL2 dependence of conditioned-medium chemotaxis.
Downstream
Immune-Mediated Periductal Inflammation The cell-culture recruitment mechanism may amplify inflammation; its necessity in vivo remains unresolved.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:20234283 SUPPORT DIRECT In Vitro
"addition of anti-Mip2/Cxcl2 antibodies depleted this chemokine in the conditioned media and completely prevented neutrophil chemotaxis."
Conditioned-medium depletion experiment; the causal intervention is in vitro.
B-Cell-Dependent Inflammatory Amplification
B-cell-deficient mice resist RRV-induced biliary obstruction and have reduced Th1 inflammation. Treg depletion does not abolish this protection. Antigen presentation is a proposed explanation; this experiment does not establish that autoantibodies themselves cause human disease.
B cell CL:0000236 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves B cell (CL:0000236). CL:0000236 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Downstream
Immune-Mediated Periductal Inflammation B-cell deficiency limits model inflammatory injury; the responsible B-cell function is incompletely defined.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23991203 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"the RRV-infected Ig-α(-/-) mice did not have hyperbilirubinemia or bile duct obstruction."
Genetic B-cell deficiency protects against model pathology.
Neutrophil Extracellular Trap Formation
CD177-positive neutrophils in the RRV model exhibit increased oxidative activity and NET formation; coculture experiments link NET-associated activity to biliary epithelial apoptosis. Cd177 loss reduces experimental disease severity. Human NAC pilot observations are exploratory and do not establish durable clinical benefit. The 2025 corrigendum corrects duplicated representative flow plots, with the authors reporting unchanged underlying statistics.
neutrophil CL:0000775 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves neutrophil (CL:0000775). CL:0000775 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Animal models
Cd177-deficient RRV mouse Mouse perturbs Cd177 loss delays onset and reduces model morbidity and mortality.
Downstream
Cholangiocyte Injury of the Bile Duct Epithelium NET-associated coculture activity damages biliary epithelium.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35803543 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"Cd177-/- BALB/c mice exhibited delayed disease onset and reduced morbidity and mortality."
Mouse genetic intervention.
PMID:35803543 SUPPORT DIRECT In Vitro
"these cells were associated with increased levels of reactive oxygen species and increased NET formation, which induced the apoptosis of biliary epithelial cells in cocultures."
The epithelial injury assay is in coculture, distinct from the in vivo mouse outcome.
Ductular Epithelial Expansion
Expanded portal biliary epithelium is a characteristic tissue response. A 2025 human single-cell study found cells coexpressing hepatocyte and cholangiocyte markers; RNA velocity and pseudotime inferred a hepatocyte-to-biliary trajectory. These computational trajectories are not lineage tracing, and their downstream fibrogenic function remains partly inferential.

Biliary Cirrhotic Remodeling
Persistent biliary injury and matrix deposition distort liver architecture into irregular regenerative nodules and scarring. Progression can continue despite restored extrahepatic drainage.
Downstream
Portal Venous Pressure Elevation Distorted hepatic architecture contributes to increased portal resistance.
Biliary cirrhosis Biliary cirrhosis is a clinical consequence or manifestation of this process; expression varies with disease stage and subtype.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32518681 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Biliary pattern cirrhosis with irregular nodules are observed on microscopy."
Architectural endpoint seen in explanted livers.
Portal Venous Pressure Elevation
Fibrotic and cirrhotic remodeling increases resistance to portal flow, producing portal hypertension and its splenic, variceal and fluid-retention complications.

Portal hypertension Portal hypertension is a clinical consequence or manifestation of this process; expression varies with disease stage and subtype.

Disease-specific clinical-pathologic association.
Impaired Intestinal Lipid Absorption
Reduced intestinal bile delivery impairs fat and fat-soluble-vitamin absorption. Nutritional deficiency and increased metabolic demands contribute to poor growth; vitamin K depletion may impair hemostasis.

Cholestatic malabsorption in the BA care guideline.
Post-Kasai Ascending Biliary Infection
The altered biliary-enteric anatomy after Kasai permits ascending infection of the remaining ducts. This secondary treatment-associated complication is distinct from hypotheses about the initiating cause of biliary atresia.
Downstream

Clinical review explicitly relates altered anatomy to postoperative cholangitis.
✶

Histopathology

3
Portal ductular reaction
Proliferating and anastomosing bile ductules at expanded portal-tract margins support large-duct obstruction. They are not unique to biliary atresia, and early biopsies may be nonspecific. An exact human histopathology ontology binding is still needed; the mouse/rat hyperplasia terms are not appropriate substitutes.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32518681 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Portal tracts show expansion and demonstrate edematous fibroplasia with bile ductular proliferation, consisting of anastomosing ductules located at the periphery of the portal tracts"
Characteristic portal architecture in the pathology review.
Bile plugs in ductules
Inspissated bile within ductular lumina is a useful obstructive histologic finding, interpreted alongside the clinical workup. A compatible histopathology ontology binding is needed; the specific NCIT Bile Plug concept is outside the current permitted hierarchy.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32518681 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Ductular cholestasis (inspissated bile plugs within ductular lumen) is commonly seen and represent a useful diagnostic feature."
Direct microscopic finding described in the review.
Periportal fibrosis
Portal fibroplasia can progress to bridging fibrosis and biliary cirrhotic remodeling. Histologic severity reflects disease stage but is not an infallible predictor of native-liver survival.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32518681 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Higher stages of portal fibrosis have been associated with more severe portal hypertension and increased risk of LT"
Pathology review also notes conflicting prognostic studies.

Medical Actions

14
Kasai Hepatoportoenterostomy
Action: surgical procedureNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is surgical procedure (NCIT:C15329). NCIT:C15329 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Surgical Procedure NCIT:C15329
Platform: Surgery
Time-sensitive surgery excises the fibrotic extrahepatic remnant and connects a Roux-en-Y jejunal limb to the porta hepatis to establish bile drainage. Earlier intervention generally improves outcomes; age alone is not a universal cutoff for eligibility. Progressive intrahepatic disease can persist despite jaundice clearance, requiring long-term follow-up.
Mechanism Target:
BYPASSES Fibro-obliteration of the Biliary Tree — The portoenterostomy surgically bypasses the obliterated extrahepatic biliary tree, re-establishing bile drainage from the porta hepatis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36051179 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"In the 1950s, with the development of Kasai portoenterostomy by Dr. Morio Kasai, it became possible to treat BA surgically"
Surgical drainage bypasses the extrahepatic obstruction.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36051179 SUPPORT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"BA is always treated surgically, and performing the surgery before the age of 2 mo greatly increases its effectiveness and extends the time until the need for liver transplantation arises."
Establishes the Kasai portoenterostomy as first-line surgery and its strong age-dependence (best before ~2 months).
Liver Transplantation
Action: liver transplantationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is liver transplantation (NCIT:C15271). NCIT:C15271 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Liver Transplantation NCIT:C15271
Platform: Surgery
Indicated after failed drainage or progressive liver disease, including decompensated cirrhosis, refractory portal-hypertensive complications, severe growth failure or recurrent severe cholangitis. Selected infants with advanced disease may undergo primary transplantation. Assessment depends on the clinical course, not a single universal age or laboratory threshold.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42569433 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Indications for LT after KPE include poor bile drainage and unresolved jaundice, poor bile flow in the early stages, advanced development of cirrhosis despite good bile flow."
Consensus guidance describes post-Kasai transplant indications.
Ursodeoxycholic Acid
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: ursodeoxycholic acid CHEBI:9907 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses ursodeoxycholic acid (CHEBI:9907). CHEBI:9907 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Commonly used empirically after Kasai to improve bile flow and biochemical indices. Evidence for improved native-liver survival is insufficient; dosing and duration vary between centers.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES Impaired Bile Drainage — As a choleretic bile acid, UDCA promotes bile flow and shifts the bile-acid pool to less hepatotoxic species, mitigating the cholestatic node.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42569433 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Currently, the use of these medications is largely empirical, with different medical facilities adopting varying dosing schedules. There are no definitive guidelines on when to discontinue treatment"
The guideline describes empirical use of bile-acid-modulating medication after Kasai.
Prophylactic Antibiotics for Cholangitis
Action: antibiotic therapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is antibiotic therapy (NCIT:C15620). NCIT:C15620 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Antibiotic Therapy NCIT:C15620
Post-Kasai prophylaxis is common and conditionally recommended in some guidance, but studies conflict and a reliable reduction in cholangitis or improved native-liver survival has not been established. Prophylaxis must be distinguished from treatment of a suspected acute episode.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:42569433 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Studies have produced conflicting results on the effectiveness of antibiotics in preventing postoperative cholangitis after KPE"
Evidence for preventive antibiotics is uncertain.
Fat-Soluble Vitamin and Nutritional Support
Action: nutritional supportNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is nutritional support (NCIT:C15433). NCIT:C15433 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. Ontology label: Nutritional Support NCIT:C15433
Platform: Other
Provide individualized energy-dense feeding, including medium-chain triglycerides while retaining essential long-chain fats, and monitor growth and fat-soluble vitamins A, D, E and K. Replace deficiencies and use tube feeding or, when necessary, parenteral nutrition if growth remains inadequate.

Post-Kasai Corticosteroid Therapy
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: prednisolone CHEBI:8378 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses prednisolone (CHEBI:8378). CHEBI:8378 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Randomized trial findings differ. The multicenter double-blind START trial (140 infants) did not significantly improve six-month bile drainage or two-year native-liver survival and produced earlier serious adverse events. A later single-center open-label randomized trial (200 type III cases) reported improved jaundice clearance and native-liver survival with 10–12 weeks of steroids. Differences in regimen, population and study design limit a universal treatment conclusion; use remains center-dependent.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:24794368 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"high-dose steroid therapy following surgery did not result in statistically significant treatment differences in bile drainage at 6 months, although a small clinical benefit could not be excluded."
START did not demonstrate a significant benefit for its tested regimen; its authors could not exclude a smaller clinical effect.
PMID:35170539 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"The proportion of participants that are jaundice free without liver transplantation was significantly higher in the steroid group than in the control group at 6 months (54.1% vs 31.0%, P = 0.0015)."
Later open-label randomized evidence differs from START; this does not establish equal benefit across regimens or populations.
Maralixibat (IBAT Inhibitor)
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: maralixibat CHEBI:748720 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses maralixibat (CHEBI:748720). CHEBI:748720 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Investigational in biliary atresia. The completed 75-participant EMBARK randomized trial did not improve its primary 26-week total-bilirubin endpoint versus placebo. A separate uncontrolled six-patient compassionate-use series reported pruritus improvement, with inconsistent biochemical responses and two subsequent transplants. Symptomatic observations in selected children do not establish disease modification or native-liver survival benefit.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES Impaired Bile Drainage — IBAT inhibition reduces enterohepatic bile-acid recycling as a pharmacologic rationale. EMBARK did not establish a clinical improvement in post-Kasai cholestasis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32653991 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"By reducing return of bile acids to the cholestatic liver, IBAT inhibitors may potentially lessen or delay liver damage associated with the hepatotoxicity and cholangiopathy of bile acid accumulation."
A proposed mechanism, not proof of clinical efficacy in biliary atresia.

N-Acetylcysteine (post-Kasai)
Action: PharmacotherapyNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Pharmacotherapy (NCIT:C15986). NCIT:C15986 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15986
Agent: N-acetyl-L-cysteine CHEBI:28939 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses N-acetyl-L-cysteine (CHEBI:28939). CHEBI:28939 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Experimental glutathione-precursor/choleretic strategy. A nonrandomized phase II pilot tested intravenous NAC for seven days after Kasai; none of 12 evaluable infants achieved total serum bile acids ≤10 µmol/L within 24 weeks, so the second stage did not open. Secondary outcomes did not differ from matched historical controls. One enrolled infant stopped treatment for worsening preexisting tachycardia, a serious event considered possibly drug-related in the full text. Longer courses remain unproven. A separate small Chinese pilot (ChiCTR2000040505; six treated and six untreated BA infants in the transcriptomic analysis) reported short-term bilirubin and immune-signature changes. This exploratory study used a different perioperative schedule and does not establish long-term clinical efficacy or contradict the US trial’s failure to meet its prespecified endpoint.
Show evidence (5 references)
PMID:40489761 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"There were 12 participants in stage 1 who received treatment, with none achieving TSBAs ≤10 μmol/L within 24 weeks of KP. As a result, no participants were enrolled in stage 2."
The stringent primary endpoint was not met; this does not establish efficacy for other schedules.
PMID:40489761 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"One serious adverse event was considered possibly related to the drug. It involved worsening tachycardia in a participant who had preexisting tachycardia."
The full text provides a safety qualification absent from the broad abstract summary.
PMID:40489761 SUPPORT DIRECT BACKGROUND Human Clinical
"NAC provides the amino acid cysteine, which drives production of the anti-oxidative and anti-inflammatory molecule glutathione."
The trial rationale concerns glutathione availability; it did not test correction of a CD177-specific neutrophil mechanism.

Environmental Factors

3
Perinatal hepatotropic viral infection
viral infection ECTO:3000001 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is viral infection, annotated with exposure to virus (ECTO:3000001). ECTO:3000001 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Viral exposure is a proposed trigger, not an established cause of human biliary atresia. Neonatal RRV infection induces an age-dependent obstructive cholangiopathy in mice. Human detection of viruses such as CMV is heterogeneous and cannot alone establish whether infection caused, followed, or modified the biliary disease.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23844248 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"Early RRV infection induced symptoms in 95% of pups with a mortality rate of 80%. In contrast, late infection caused symptoms in only 50% of mice, and 100% of pups survived."
Experimental susceptibility depends on neonatal timing; this does not establish human viral causation.
Biliatresone toxin exposure
biliatresone toxin exposure ECTO:0000537 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is biliatresone toxin exposure, annotated with exposure to toxin (ECTO:0000537). ECTO:0000537 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Biliatresone is an experimental electrophilic toxin that selectively damages extrahepatic cholangiocytes in zebrafish. Human exposure as a cause of biliary atresia is unproven. Low-dose prenatal exposure in mice altered offspring bile-acid profiles and hepatic immune cells without producing histologic duct injury or fibrosis at the measured time points.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:27102575 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Biliatresone has selective toxicity for extrahepatic cholangiocytes (EHCs) in zebrafish larvae."
Biliatresone selectively injures extrahepatic cholangiocytes in zebrafish larvae; human etiologic relevance is unproven.
PMID:38578745 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"Pups had no evidence of histological liver or bile duct injury or fibrosis at either timepoint. In addition, growth was normal."
Prenatal low-dose model produced biochemical and immune changes rather than overt fibro-obliterative atresia.
PMID:38578745 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"serum levels of glycocholic acid were elevated at P5, suggesting altered bile metabolism, and the serum bile acid profile became increasingly abnormal through P21"
Measured offspring bile-acid phenotype after prenatal exposure.
Prenatal maternal infection
A Taiwanese administrative-data case-control study associated recorded maternal intestinal and genitourinary infections with offspring biliary atresia. Diagnoses were code-based, pathogens were usually unspecified, and unmeasured genetic and environmental confounding remained. The findings do not demonstrate fetal infection, a causal injury pathway, or a prevention effect.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38170523 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Results of this case-control study indicate an association between prenatal intestinal infection and genitourinary tract infection in mothers and BA occurrence in their offspring."
A large Taiwanese case-control study links prenatal maternal gut and genitourinary infection to offspring biliary atresia risk.

Biochemical Markers

4
Elevated conjugated (direct) bilirubin (Elevated)
Context: Conjugated/direct hyperbilirubinemia requires urgent evaluation for neonatal cholestasis, including biliary atresia. After the neonatal screening window, a direct bilirubin above 1 mg/dL warrants evaluation regardless of its percentage of total bilirubin. In the first days of life, use age- and assay-specific reference intervals; an absolute 1 mg/dL screening cutoff can miss affected newborns. Direct and conjugated assays are not identical.
Show evidence (2 references)
url:https://naspghan.org/files/documents/pdfs/position-papers/Guideline_for_the_Evaluation_of_Cholestatic.23.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"because it is physiologically and clinically complex to consider incorporating consideration of whether or not the direct fraction exceeds 20% of the TB level as mentioned in some publications"
The 2017 NASPGHAN/ESPGHAN guideline defines abnormal direct/conjugated bilirubin independently of the former 20% rule.
url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/7093763/ SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"1 infant with a true positive screening result had a stage 1 bilirubin level of 0.4 mg/dL, which was abnormally high in the 98.4th to 99.6th percentile for that site"
The primary screening study demonstrates why newborn thresholds must be age- and laboratory-specific.
Elevated gamma-glutamyltransferase (GGT) (Elevated)
Context: GGT is commonly elevated, often disproportionately to aminotransferases, but varies with age and cannot establish or exclude biliary atresia alone.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:36051179 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Transaminases and gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT) are almost always elevated in BA, and GGT is disproportionately higher than transaminases, while this is opposite in neonatal hepatitis."
Clinical review describes the typical biochemical pattern; neither enzyme is diagnostic alone.
Elevated circulating hepatic transaminases (Elevated)
Context: ALT and AST can be elevated with accompanying hepatocellular injury. Values are nonspecific, and early biochemical screening may identify affected newborns before aminotransferases rise.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:36051179 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Transaminases and gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT) are almost always elevated in BA, and GGT is disproportionately higher than transaminases, while this is opposite in neonatal hepatitis."
Clinical review describes the typical biochemical pattern; neither enzyme is diagnostic alone.
url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/7093763/ SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"biliary atresia, which can be difficult to detect during early stages of the disease when aminotransferase levels and liver stiffness may still be normal"
Normal early aminotransferases must not delay assessment of abnormal direct/conjugated bilirubin.
Elevated serum matrix metalloproteinase-7 (MMP7) (Elevated)
Context: Serum MMP-7 is a promising adjunct for distinguishing biliary atresia from other neonatal cholestases. Thresholds depend on assay and age: the North American study used 52.8 ng/mL on a single-plex assay and 18.2 ng/mL on a different platform. A separate prospective ELISA cohort validated 18 ng/mL with 95.9% sensitivity and 87.3% specificity. False-negative and false-positive results occur; these thresholds are not interchangeable or a substitute for definitive assessment.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38446707 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"Serum concentrations produced by time resolved fluorescence energy transfer differed from single-plex, with an optimal cutoff of 18.2 ng/mL. Results were consistent within each assay technology and generated similar AUROCs."
The same clinical sample set yielded different thresholds with different assay platforms.
PMID:38978022 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"The performance of MMP-7 was successfully validated in the larger prospective cohort, resulting in a diagnostic sensitivity of 95.9% (379/395; 95% CI: 93.5-97.7%), a specificity of 87.3% (255/292; 95% CI: 83.0-90.9%)"
Prospective validation of the 18 ng/mL ELISA threshold; incomplete sensitivity and specificity preclude an absolute rule-out or rule-in interpretation.
🔬

Diagnosis

7
Abdominal ultrasonography (triangular cord sign)
First-line imaging evaluates gallbladder morphology and contraction, the triangular cord sign and associated malformations. Findings are operator- and age-dependent; a normal ultrasound does not exclude biliary atresia. The original small study reported 85% sensitivity and 100% specificity for its triangular cord assessment, which should not be generalized to every setting.
abdominal ultrasonography NCIT:C17230 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:9396524 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Although 17 of 20 ultrasonographic examinations from infants who had BA denoted TC, 43 ultrasonographic examinations from infants with either neonatal hepatitis (NH) or other causes of cholestasis denoted no TC, showing a diagnostic accuracy of 95% with 85% sensitivity and 100% specificity."
The triangular cord sign on ultrasound is highly specific for biliary atresia in the workup of infantile cholestasis.
PMID:36051179 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Most, but not all of those with BA have a hypoplastic or absent gallbladder, and so normal ultrasonography does not exclude BA."
A normal gallbladder or ultrasound cannot exclude the disease.
Hepatobiliary scintigraphy (HIDA)
Hepatobiliary scintigraphy is sensitive but has limited specificity: absent bowel tracer excretion also occurs in other cholestases. Demonstrated excretion makes biliary atresia unlikely, but rare apparent false-negative studies are reported. It should not delay definitive evaluation; routine phenobarbital pretreatment can add several days.
Show evidence (2 references)
url:https://naspghan.org/files/documents/pdfs/position-papers/Guideline_for_the_Evaluation_of_Cholestatic.23.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Limited reports describe infants with apparently initially excreting HBS and a subsequent diagnosis of BA, although the technical limitations of the study may have been a factor in its utility"
The guideline documents rare apparent excretion despite subsequent biliary-atresia diagnosis.
url:https://naspghan.org/files/documents/pdfs/position-papers/Guideline_for_the_Evaluation_of_Cholestatic.23.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Many clinicians and radiologists administer phenobarbital for 5 days before the study, in an attempt to enhance biliary excretion of the isotope and increase its discriminatory value"
Premedication may delay an already time-sensitive diagnostic pathway.
Intraoperative cholangiography
Operative cholangiography, interpreted with duct-remnant histology and the clinical workup, is the reference standard for defining extrahepatic duct patency. If biliary atresia is confirmed, Kasai portoenterostomy can usually follow during the same operation. A hypoplastic biliary tree in Alagille syndrome or cystic fibrosis can confound interpretation; cholangiography is not infallible.
intraoperative cholangiography NCIT:C61508 NCI Thesaurus (NCIT)
Show evidence (2 references)
url:https://naspghan.org/files/documents/pdfs/position-papers/Guideline_for_the_Evaluation_of_Cholestatic.23.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The intraoperative cholangiogram and histologic examin- ation of the duct remnant is considered the criterion standard to diagnose BA"
The guideline combines operative anatomy with remnant histology.
url:https://naspghan.org/files/documents/pdfs/position-papers/Guideline_for_the_Evaluation_of_Cholestatic.23.pdf SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Hence, preoperative testing for CF and ALGS is helpful in assisting in the interpretation ... and decreasing false-positive results."
Clinical mimics affect operative interpretation.
Infant stool color card screening
Parents compare infant stool color with a reference card and promptly report pale stools. National programs support earlier recognition, but stools may initially retain pigment. Taiwanese before-and-after cohort findings associate screening-era care with lower hospitalization and mortality; this observational design cannot isolate screening from other changes in care.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:27015203 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Since 2004, Taiwan has implemented a national screening program that uses an infant stool color card (SCC) for the early detection of BA."
Stool color card screening is a validated national program for early detection of biliary atresia.
PMID:36051179 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Acholic stool may initially be absent and some stools may contain bile pigment"
Early pigmented stools do not exclude biliary atresia.
Newborn direct or conjugated bilirubin screening
A two-stage strategy measures direct/conjugated bilirubin in the first 60 hours using laboratory-specific newborn reference intervals, then repeats abnormal results by the two-week visit. Rising values or repeat values above 1 mg/dL prompt further assessment. In 124,385 newborns, all seven known cases were detected, but sensitivity had a wide confidence interval and false-negative ascertainment was incomplete. A separate pre-post analysis associated implementation with younger Kasai age, not proof of a survival benefit.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:32207797 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"In stage 1, all newborns were tested within the first 60 hours of life, with a positive screening result defined as bilirubin levels exceeding derived 95th percentile reference intervals."
First-stage screening as summarized in the abstract; full methods describe site-specific newborn reference intervals.
PMID:32207797 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"In stage 2, infants who had a positive screening result in stage 1 were retested at or before the 2-week well-child visit, with a positive screening result defined as bilirubin levels greater than the stage 1 result or greater than 1 mg/dL."
Second-stage screen requires a rise or a value above 1 mg/dL.
PMID:32207797 SUPPORT DIRECT Human Clinical
"Newborn screening with direct or conjugated bilirubin measurements detected all known infants with biliary atresia in the study population, although the 95% CI around the sensitivity estimate was wide and the study design did not ensure complete ascertainment of false-negative results."
The abstract explicitly qualifies sensitivity and incomplete ascertainment.
Primary-care assessment at two to four weeks
The 2025 AAP clinical report recommends assessing eye color, stool color and prior laboratory results at the two- to four-week visit to decide whether direct/conjugated bilirubin measurement is needed. Persistent jaundice or pale stools requires prompt evaluation.
Show evidence (1 reference)
DOI:10.1542/peds.2024-070077 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"The strategy involves examining every infant’s eye color, stool color, and prior laboratory results to determine whether measurement of a direct or conjugated bilirubin level is warranted."
AAP clinical report, abstract available; full report retrieval was blocked.
Liver biopsy
Portal expansion, ductular reaction and bile plugs support biliary obstruction, but early biopsy can be nonspecific and similar changes occur in other neonatal cholestases. Interpret expert histology alongside imaging and clinical evaluation rather than using a negative early biopsy to exclude biliary atresia.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:32518681 SUPPORT DIRECT REVIEW SYNTHESIS Human Clinical
"Histopathological findings in liver biopsies include the expansion of the portal tracts, with edematous fibroplasia and bile ductular proliferation, with bile plugs in duct lumen."
Disease-associated obstructive histology, interpreted in clinical context.

Experimental Models

2
H69 ADD3 regulatory-element reporter assays CELL_LINE
Allelic fragments from the ADD3 susceptibility interval were cloned into reporters. Four showed differing haplotype activity, whereas the fragment containing the lead marker did not.
Cell source
Immortalized human H69 cholangiocytes
Publication
PMID:41589307
Pathograph links
Altered ADD3 Regulatory Activity measures fidelity: moderate Reports allelic transcriptional activity of cloned DNA fragments.
Limitations: Does not measure native enhancer-promoter contacts or endogenous expression in patients.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41589307 SUPPORT DIRECT In Vitro
"four elements (E2, E4, E9 and E10) showed allele-specific reporter activity differences"
Direct reporter measurement in the H69 human cholangiocyte cell line.
RRV-infected macrophage conditioned-medium assay CELL_LINE
Conditioned medium from infected macrophages stimulates neutrophil chemotaxis; CXCL2 depletion abolishes the measured response.
Cell source
Murine RAW264.7 macrophages and isolated neutrophils
Publication
PMID:20234283
Pathograph links
Macrophage CXCL2-Dependent Neutrophil Chemotaxis perturbs fidelity: moderate Tests the CXCL2 dependence of conditioned-medium chemotaxis.
Limitations: Does not demonstrate necessity for injury in an intact animal or a human infant.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:20234283 SUPPORT DIRECT In Vitro
"addition of anti-Mip2/Cxcl2 antibodies depleted this chemokine in the conditioned media and completely prevented neutrophil chemotaxis."
Conditioned-medium depletion experiment; the causal intervention is in vitro.

Animal Models

11
Rhesus rotavirus (RRV) neonatal mouse model
Neonatal BALB/c infection with RRV produces age-dependent epithelial injury, inflammation and extrahepatic obstruction. Early and late infection have substantially different outcomes. This defined viral model tests selected injury mechanisms; it does not establish the cause or all subtypes of human disease.
Species
Mouse
Genotype
Wild-type BALB/c neonate infected with rhesus rotavirus
Publication
PMID:23844248
Pathograph links
Cholangiocyte Injury of the Bile Duct Epithelium partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate Early infection produces biliary epithelial injury and obstruction.
Limitations: Susceptibility depends on mouse age and viral conditions; BASM developmental defects and human etiologic heterogeneity are not reproduced.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23844248 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"the amount of cholangiocyte epithelial damage and bile duct edema was significantly greater in the early infection group"
Histologic epithelial injury in the time-dependent RRV mouse model.
Biliatresone zebrafish model
Exposure of zebrafish larvae to the plant toxin biliatresone selectively injures the extrahepatic biliary epithelium, providing a toxin-driven model of the extrahepatic cholangiopathy of biliary atresia.
Species
Zebrafish
Genotype
Wild-type larvae exposed to the isoflavonoid toxin biliatresone
Publication
PMID:27102575
Pathograph links
Cholangiocyte Injury of the Bile Duct Epithelium recapitulates fidelity: moderate Biliatresone reproduces selective extrahepatic-cholangiocyte injury, modeling the toxin route to the cholangiocyte-injury node.
Limitations: Zebrafish biliary anatomy differs from the human extrahepatic tree, and the specific human relevance of biliatresone exposure remains unproven; the model captures the injury mechanism rather than human etiology.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27102575 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Biliatresone has selective toxicity for extrahepatic cholangiocytes (EHCs) in zebrafish larvae."
Demonstrates selective extrahepatic-cholangiocyte toxicity, grounding the toxin model of the injury node.
Reduced Cholangiocyte Glutathione Reserve perturbs fidelity: moderate Glutathione depletion increases toxin susceptibility, while antioxidant manipulations reduce injury.
Limitations: Experimental toxin exposure is not a demonstrated cause of human biliary atresia.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:27102575 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"GSH depletion sensitized both EHCs and the otherwise resistant intrahepatic cholangiocytes to the toxin"
Manipulating antioxidant reserve changes susceptibility in fish.
Gpc1 knockdown zebrafish
Knockdown impairs the biliary network; gpc1 mRNA rescue and partial cyclopamine rescue support specificity and Hedgehog involvement.
Species
Zebrafish
Genotype
gpc1 morpholino knockdown
Publication
PMID:23336978
Pathograph links
Disrupted Biliary Morphogenesis perturbs fidelity: moderate Knockdown impairs the biliary network; gpc1 mRNA rescue and partial cyclopamine rescue support specificity and Hedgehog involvement.
Limitations: Morpholino perturbation is not a human deletion genotype; the model does not demonstrate penetrance or progressive human remnant fibrosis.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:23336978 SUPPORT DIRECT Model Organism
"Knockdown of gpc1 in zebrafish led to developmental biliary defects."
Experimental biliary developmental phenotype.

Independent dosage perturbations provide a plausible bridge to biliary development, not the effect of the assayed human variants in their native locus.

Mutants have laterality abnormalities, sparse intrahepatic biliary networks and impaired biliary transport; most retain a gallbladder.

Offspring showed bile-acid and immune changes without histologic liver or duct injury or fibrosis at the measured neonatal time points.

Source YAML

click to show 
name: Biliary Atresia
creation_date: '2026-08-12T00:00:00Z'
category: Complex
disease_term:
  preferred_term: biliary atresia
  term:
    id: MONDO:0008867
    label: biliary atresia
description: >-
  Biliary atresia is an infantile fibro-obliterative cholangiopathy that obstructs
  bile drainage and causes
  conjugated hyperbilirubinemia, pale stools and progressive liver disease. Injury
  commonly begins before visible
  jaundice, and abnormal direct/conjugated bilirubin can be present shortly after
  birth. Clinical presentations
  include isolated disease, laterality-associated malformations and cystic biliary
  remnants. Genetic susceptibility,
  developmental abnormalities and dysregulated immune responses contribute to mechanistic
  models, while specific
  viral or toxin causes remain unproven in humans. Kasai portoenterostomy can restore
  drainage and prolong
  native-liver survival, but many patients develop continuing fibrosis, portal hypertension
  or recurrent cholangitis
  and require liver transplantation.

parents:
- Neonatal Cholestasis

classifications:
  harrisons_chapter:
  - classification_value: GASTROINTESTINAL
has_subtypes:
- name: Isolated BA
  display_name: Isolated (non-syndromic) biliary atresia
  description: >-
    Biliary atresia without a recognized associated malformation syndrome, comprising
    most cases. The historical
    term perinatal form describes presentation, not proof of postnatal acquisition:
    direct/conjugated bilirubin
    may already be abnormal in the first days of life.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34884377
    reference_title: 'Biliary Atresia: Clinical Phenotypes and Aetiological Heterogeneity.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL

    snippet: >-

      few clues as to aetiology
      or indeed pathogenesis.

      Review synthesis of this clinical subgroup; definitions and overlap vary.

- name: BASM
  display_name: Biliary atresia splenic malformation syndrome
  description: >-
    Biliary atresia with splenic and other laterality-associated malformations, including
    polysplenia or asplenia,
    abnormal visceral situs, intestinal malrotation, preduodenal portal vein and interrupted
    inferior vena
    cava. Definitions vary between cohorts. It accounts for roughly 10–15% in some
    Western series but is much
    less frequent in some Asian series; prognosis depends on associated anatomy and
    clinical context.

- name: CBA
  display_name: Cystic biliary atresia
  description: >-
    Biliary atresia with cystic dilatation of part of the extrahepatic remnant, sometimes
    detected prenatally.
    It must be distinguished from a choledochal cyst. Timely Kasai surgery often gives
    better jaundice-clearance
    outcomes than other biliary-atresia variants, although late deterioration can
    still occur.

inheritance:
- name: Non-Mendelian / multifactorial
  description: >-
    Usually sporadic and non-Mendelian. A systematic review of 35 reported twin pairs
    found 34 discordant pairs,
    but published cases do not provide a population recurrence or penetrance estimate.
    Common susceptibility
    alleles and rare candidate variants may contribute. Biallelic PKD1L1 variants
    in five of 67 broadly defined
    BASM cases support a candidate recessive contribution in some patients, not an
    established Mendelian explanation
    for BASM as a whole.

- name: Fibro-obliteration of the Biliary Tree
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  conforms_to: fibrotic_response#Excessive ECM Deposition
  description: >-
    Activated portal myofibroblasts deposit excess extracellular matrix around and
    within the injured ducts, producing a fibro-obliterative process that
    progressively narrows and obliterates the extrahepatic biliary tree and damages
    the intrahepatic ducts, forming the characteristic fibrous remnant at the porta
    hepatis. This obliteration is the anatomic substrate that the Kasai
    portoenterostomy attempts to bypass.
  biological_processes:

      fibroplasia and bile ductular proliferation, with bile plugs in duct lumen.
    explanation: >-
      Portal-tract fibroplasia and ductular reaction on biopsy evidence the
      fibro-obliterative remodeling of this node.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS

- name: Embryonic Patterning Disturbance

- name: Excess Hedgehog Signaling in Developmental Models
  description: >-

- name: Reduced Cholangiocyte Glutathione Reserve

phenotypes:
- name: Conjugated hyperbilirubinemia
  description: >-
    Elevation of the direct (conjugated) bilirubin fraction due to obstructed bile
    flow; the biochemical hallmark of neonatal cholestasis and the finding that
    mandates urgent evaluation for biliary atresia.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Conjugated hyperbilirubinemia
    term:
      id: HP:0002908
      label: Conjugated hyperbilirubinemia

  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36051179
    reference_title: Biliary atresia and congenital disorders of the extrahepatic bile ducts.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: >-
      Biliary atresia (BA) and choledochal cysts are known to cause obstructive jaundice,
      and conjugated hyperbilirubinemia
      will be observed.
    explanation: >-
      The defining cholestatic biochemical phenotype is not unique to biliary atresia.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
- name: Prolonged neonatal jaundice
  description: >-
    Jaundice persisting beyond the first two weeks of life. In biliary atresia the
    jaundice is cholestatic (conjugated) rather than the benign unconjugated
    physiologic jaundice of the newborn.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Prolonged neonatal jaundice
    term:
      id: HP:0006579
      label: Prolonged neonatal jaundice
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34884377
    reference_title: 'Biliary Atresia: Clinical Phenotypes and Aetiological Heterogeneity.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Biliary atresia (BA) is an obliterative condition of the biliary tract that
      presents with persistent
      jaundice and pale stools typically in the first few weeks of life.
    explanation: >-
      Persistent jaundice in the first weeks of life is the defining presenting
      feature of biliary atresia.

    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
- name: Acholic stools
  description: >-
    Pale stools reflect reduced delivery of bile pigment to the intestine. They may
    develop after an initially
    pigmented-stool period, so their early absence does not exclude biliary atresia.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Acholic stools
    term:
      id: HP:0011985
      label: Acholic stools
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34884377
    reference_title: 'Biliary Atresia: Clinical Phenotypes and Aetiological Heterogeneity.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Biliary atresia (BA) is an obliterative condition of the biliary tract that
      presents with persistent
      jaundice and pale stools typically in the first few weeks of life.
    explanation: >-
      Pale (acholic) stools are part of the defining presenting signature of
      biliary atresia, reflecting absent bile pigment in the gut.

    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS

biochemical:
- name: Elevated conjugated (direct) bilirubin
  presence: Elevated
  context: >-
    Conjugated/direct hyperbilirubinemia requires urgent evaluation for neonatal cholestasis,
    including biliary
    atresia. After the neonatal screening window, a direct bilirubin above 1 mg/dL
    warrants evaluation regardless
    of its percentage of total bilirubin. In the first days of life, use age- and
    assay-specific reference
    intervals; an absolute 1 mg/dL screening cutoff can miss affected newborns. Direct
    and conjugated assays
    are not identical.

  evidence:
  - reference: url:https://naspghan.org/files/documents/pdfs/position-papers/Guideline_for_the_Evaluation_of_Cholestatic.23.pdf
    reference_title: https://naspghan.org/files/documents/pdfs/position-papers/Guideline_for_the_Evaluation_of_Cholestatic.23.pdf
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: >-
      because it is physiologically and clinically complex to consider incorporating
      consideration of whether
      or not the direct fraction exceeds 20% of the TB level as mentioned in some
      publications
    explanation: >-
      The 2017 NASPGHAN/ESPGHAN guideline defines abnormal direct/conjugated bilirubin
      independently of the
      former 20% rule.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS
  - reference: url:https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/7093763/
    reference_title: Diagnostic Yield of Newborn Screening for Biliary Atresia Using Direct or Conjugated Bilirubin Measurements - PMC
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: >-

      of 0.4 mg/dL, which was
      abnormally high in the 98.4th to 99.6th percentile for that site
    explanation: >-
      The primary screening study demonstrates why newborn thresholds must be age-
      and laboratory-specific.
- name: Elevated gamma-glutamyltransferase (GGT)
  presence: Elevated
  context: >-
    GGT is commonly elevated, often disproportionately to aminotransferases, but varies
    with age and cannot
    establish or exclude biliary atresia alone.

  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:36051179
    reference_title: Biliary atresia and congenital disorders of the extrahepatic bile ducts.
    supports: SUPPORT

    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: >-
      Transaminases and gamma-glutamyl transferase (GGT) are almost always elevated
      in BA, and GGT is disproportionately
      higher than transaminases, while this is opposite in neonatal hepatitis.
    explanation: >-
      Clinical review describes the typical biochemical pattern; neither enzyme is
      diagnostic alone.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS

  presence: Elevated
  context: >-

    nonspecific, and early
    biochemical screening may identify affected newborns before aminotransferases
    rise.

- name: Elevated serum matrix metalloproteinase-7 (MMP7)
  presence: Elevated
  context: >-
    Serum MMP-7 is a promising adjunct for distinguishing biliary atresia from other
    neonatal cholestases.
    Thresholds depend on assay and age: the North American study used 52.8 ng/mL on
    a single-plex assay and
    18.2 ng/mL on a different platform. A separate prospective ELISA cohort validated
    18 ng/mL with 95.9% sensitivity
    and 87.3% specificity. False-negative and false-positive results occur; these
    thresholds are not interchangeable
    or a substitute for definitive assessment.

  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38446707
    reference_title: Diagnostic accuracy of serum matrix metalloproteinase-7 as a biomarker of biliary atresia in a large North American cohort.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: >-
      Serum concentrations produced by time resolved fluorescence energy transfer
      differed from single-plex,
      with an optimal cutoff of 18.2 ng/mL. Results were consistent within each assay
      technology and generated
      similar AUROCs.
    explanation: >-
      The same clinical sample set yielded different thresholds with different assay
      platforms.
  - reference: PMID:38978022
    reference_title: 'Serum matrix metalloproteinase-7 for discriminating biliary atresia: a diagnostic accuracy and validation study.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: >-
      The performance of MMP-7 was successfully validated in the larger prospective
      cohort, resulting in a
      diagnostic sensitivity of 95.9% (379/395; 95% CI: 93.5-97.7%), a specificity
      of 87.3% (255/292; 95% CI:
      83.0-90.9%)
    explanation: >-
      Prospective validation of the 18 ng/mL ELISA threshold; incomplete sensitivity
      and specificity preclude
      an absolute rule-out or rule-in interpretation.
diagnosis:
- name: Abdominal ultrasonography (triangular cord sign)
  description: >-
    First-line imaging evaluates gallbladder morphology and contraction, the triangular
    cord sign and associated
    malformations. Findings are operator- and age-dependent; a normal ultrasound does
    not exclude biliary atresia.
    The original small study reported 85% sensitivity and 100% specificity for its
    triangular cord assessment,
    which should not be generalized to every setting.
  diagnosis_term:
    preferred_term: abdominal ultrasonography
    term:
      id: NCIT:C17230
      label: Ultrasound Imaging
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:9396524
    reference_title: 'A new diagnostic approach to biliary atresia with emphasis on the ultrasonographic triangular cord sign: comparison of ultrasonography, hepatobiliary scintigraphy, and liver needle biopsy in the evaluation of infantile cholestasis.'
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Although 17 of 20 ultrasonographic examinations from infants who had BA denoted
      TC, 43 ultrasonographic
      examinations from infants with either neonatal hepatitis (NH) or other causes
      of cholestasis denoted
      no TC, showing a diagnostic accuracy of 95% with 85% sensitivity and 100% specificity.
    explanation: >-
      The triangular cord sign on ultrasound is highly specific for biliary
      atresia in the workup of infantile cholestasis.

    explanation: >-

- name: Hepatobiliary scintigraphy (HIDA)
  description: >-
    Hepatobiliary scintigraphy is sensitive but has limited specificity: absent bowel
    tracer excretion also
    occurs in other cholestases. Demonstrated excretion makes biliary atresia unlikely,
    but rare apparent false-negative
    studies are reported. It should not delay definitive evaluation; routine phenobarbital
    pretreatment can
    add several days.

  evidence:
  - reference: url:https://naspghan.org/files/documents/pdfs/position-papers/Guideline_for_the_Evaluation_of_Cholestatic.23.pdf

    directness: DIRECT
    snippet: >-

      a subsequent diagnosis of
      BA, although the technical limitations of the study may have been a factor in
      its utility
    explanation: >-
      The guideline documents rare apparent excretion despite subsequent biliary-atresia
      diagnosis.
    quote_role: REVIEW_SYNTHESIS

- name: Intraoperative cholangiography
  description: >-
    Operative cholangiography, interpreted with duct-remnant histology and the clinical
    workup, is the reference
    standard for defining extrahepatic duct patency. If biliary atresia is confirmed,
    Kasai portoenterostomy
    can usually follow during the same operation. A hypoplastic biliary tree in Alagille
    syndrome or cystic
    fibrosis can confound interpretation; cholangiography is not infallible.
  diagnosis_term:
    preferred_term: intraoperative cholangiography
    term:
      id: NCIT:C61508

- name: Infant stool color card screening
  description: >-
    Parents compare infant stool color with a reference card and promptly report pale
    stools. National programs
    support earlier recognition, but stools may initially retain pigment. Taiwanese
    before-and-after cohort
    findings associate screening-era care with lower hospitalization and mortality;
    this observational design
    cannot isolate screening from other changes in care.
